## Quarterfinals – Shuhei Nakamura vs. Rodrigo Renedo

*by Tobias Henke*

![](https://i.ormos.uk/ea/a3/eaa3f806c493e82915c991641ca7cc6a06546330eabed202de61576ddc76e403.jpg)Apparently, 5-color Merfolk is all the rage now; two of them even meet in the quarterfinals!

The first game started fast and furious, with Renedo's [Stonybrook Banneret](https://web.archive.org/web/20220808185906/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Stonybrook+Banneret) into [Vendilion Clique](https://web.archive.org/web/20220808185906/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Vendilion+Clique). Nakamura answered both with [Firespout](https://web.archive.org/web/20220808185906/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Firespout), but Renedo redoubled his grip on the game with double Chaneleon Colossus.

Nakamura himself only had a [Silvergill Adept](https://web.archive.org/web/20220808185906/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Silvergill+Adept) and [Jace Beleren](https://web.archive.org/web/20220808185906/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Jace+Beleren). Renedo didn't even bother with the Planeswalker, instead bringing the Japanese down to three with one mighty swing of an 8/8 Colossus and one usual-sized one.

However, Nakamura was not to be defeated that easily. He even attacked for two and then cast [Cryptic Command](https://web.archive.org/web/20220808185906/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cryptic+Command) to buy some time. Next, a combination of clever chump-blocking and [Sower of Temptation](https://web.archive.org/web/20220808185906/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sower+of+Temptation) meant a comeback for the Japanese superstar.

Renedo attacked with his one remaining Colossus and his [Mutavault](https://web.archive.org/web/20220808185906/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mutavault). When Nakamura chump-blocked the Colossus and tried to dispatch the land with [Nameless Inversion](https://web.archive.org/web/20220808185906/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Nameless+Inversion), Renedo countered and tapped all but two of his lands in the process.

Nakamura untapped, laid his eigth land... hesitated a second, but then shrugged and simply attacked with the stolen Colossus and his [Sower of Temptation](https://web.archive.org/web/20220808185906/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sower+of+Temptation) for 18.

**Shuuhei Nakamura 1 – 0 Emanuele Guisti**

![](https://i.ormos.uk/ff/c1/ffc1d9873fa8d8dac33eaabf714dda3f011abfa5d02b675e1f585bc4e4505057.jpg)This time it was Renedo bringing on the [Silvergill Adept](https://web.archive.org/web/20220808185906/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Silvergill+Adept)s while Nakamura went off to a turn-three [Chameleon Colossus](https://web.archive.org/web/20220808185906/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Chameleon+Colossus), thanks to [Stonybrook Banneret](https://web.archive.org/web/20220808185906/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Stonybrook+Banneret).

Nakamura got rid of the [Silvergill Adept](https://web.archive.org/web/20220808185906/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Silvergill+Adept)s and Renedo's [Cryptic Command](https://web.archive.org/web/20220808185906/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cryptic+Command) bounced [Chameleon Colossus](https://web.archive.org/web/20220808185906/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Chameleon+Colossus). Unfortunately, the young Spaniard couldn't do anything about Nakamura's [Jace Beleren](https://web.archive.org/web/20220808185906/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Jace+Beleren) that now followed.

For about thirteen turns the Planeswalker kept drawing cards for his master. (Well, some for his master's opponent too, of course.) Meanwhile, threats were played, countered, destroyed, bounced, or even discarded... Nothing stayed on the table, allowing Jace to “keep up the good work”.

When Nakamura finally let his Planeswalker-ally die and then called upon another for even more card-drawing, it became apparent that this game wouldn't be taking long anymore. Soon after, he dropped some [Stonybrook Banneret](https://web.archive.org/web/20220808185906/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Stonybrook+Banneret)s, [Silvergill Adept](https://web.archive.org/web/20220808185906/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Silvergill+Adept)s, a [Chameleon Colossus](https://web.archive.org/web/20220808185906/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Chameleon+Colossus), and a [Sower of Temptation](https://web.archive.org/web/20220808185906/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sower+of+Temptation). Next turn he attacked and... well...

**Shuuhei Nakamura 1 – 0 Emanuele Guisti**

## Quarterfinals – Matthias Künzler (CHE) vs. Marcello Calvetto (ITA)

*by David Sutcliffe*

![](https://i.ormos.uk/1e/7a/1e7adc520e9a99a5bbe0741993a7e9937d25c68b7a0e6acdccdaba971028a4d1.jpg)Matthias Künzler was on the verge of history, here. At Grand Prix-Copenhagen last month Tomaharo Saitou won every round of the Swiss until he lost the last round, but Künzler has gone one better and managed a draw instead of a loss. If he is able to win the Grand Prix he would become the first player to ever go undefeated on both days. Many years ago the British deckbuilding master, John Ormerod, made it into the Top-8 undefeated but failed to take the crown - having surpassed Saitou, history awaited Künzler's next move. As did the 16 year old Italian Marcello Calvetto, who was hoping to upset the odds

Künzler spent a long while thinking over his second turn before casting [Bitterblossom](https://web.archive.org/web/20220808185906/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Bitterblossom) but Calvetto had the [Broken Ambitions](https://web.archive.org/web/20220808185906/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Broken+Ambitions) ready, and when he won the clash also managed to mill away both of Künzler's [Thoughtseize](https://web.archive.org/web/20220808185906/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Thoughtseize) from his deck. It was early days but would that prove crucial? Calvetto played [Scion of Oona](https://web.archive.org/web/20220808185906/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Scion+of+Oona) but Künzler activated his [Mutavault](https://web.archive.org/web/20220808185906/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mutavault) to give himself a Faerie, then [Peppersmoke](https://web.archive.org/web/20220808185906/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Peppersmoke)d the Scion away and drew a card.

“How many cards?” asked Calvetto.

“Just seven,” replied the Swiss master calmly “You?”

“Four”

“Sounds good!”

Künzler cast a [Spellstutter Sprite](https://web.archive.org/web/20220808185906/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Spellstutter+Sprite) and began nibbling away at Calvetto's lifetotal. At the end of the turn Calvetto attempted to cast a [Mistbind Clique](https://web.archive.org/web/20220808185906/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mistbind+Clique). Künzler allowed it into play but used a [Cryptic Command](https://web.archive.org/web/20220808185906/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cryptic+Command) to bounce the [Mutavault](https://web.archive.org/web/20220808185906/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mutavault) that activated to make a Faerie for the Champion effect, and the [Mistbind Clique](https://web.archive.org/web/20220808185906/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mistbind+Clique) went away again in a puff of smoke. That had been expensive for Calvetto, but it gave him an opening and he used it to resolve a [Bitterblossom](https://web.archive.org/web/20220808185906/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Bitterblossom).

Künzler attempted to get his own [Mistbind Clique](https://web.archive.org/web/20220808185906/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mistbind+Clique) into play the next turn, but it was [Cryptic Command](https://web.archive.org/web/20220808185906/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cryptic+Command)ed and Calvetto drew a card. It was now four cards apiece in hand, and Calvetto had [Bitterblossom](https://web.archive.org/web/20220808185906/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Bitterblossom) in play as well, which definitely gave him the edge. Seizing the initiative Calvetto cast a [Thoughtseize](https://web.archive.org/web/20220808185906/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Thoughtseize) and tore away a [Cryptic Command](https://web.archive.org/web/20220808185906/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cryptic+Command) from the Swiss player's hand. Unfortunately for the young Italian he had nothing to stop Künzler playing the [Bitterblossom](https://web.archive.org/web/20220808185906/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Bitterblossom) he had topdecked seconds after [Thoughtseize](https://web.archive.org/web/20220808185906/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Thoughtseize) had finished looking at his hand. With both players now having a [Bitterblossom](https://web.archive.org/web/20220808185906/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Bitterblossom) in play the lifescores became critical, and Künzler led in that race 17 to 13.

Künzler attacked, and after blockers played a [Scion of Oona](https://web.archive.org/web/20220808185906/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Scion+of+Oona) that would change the combat maths. Calvetto countered the Scion with a [Spellstutter Sprite](https://web.archive.org/web/20220808185906/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Spellstutter+Sprite), but lost a [Mutavault](https://web.archive.org/web/20220808185906/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mutavault) to Künzler's [Nameless Inversion](https://web.archive.org/web/20220808185906/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Nameless+Inversion) and went down to 11 life, 10 in his upkeep. Both players were reduced topdecking after that exchange, and Calvetto found gold in a [Sower of Temptation](https://web.archive.org/web/20220808185906/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sower+of+Temptation) and then a [Spellstutter Sprite](https://web.archive.org/web/20220808185906/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Spellstutter+Sprite) to protect it from another [Nameless Inversion](https://web.archive.org/web/20220808185906/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Nameless+Inversion) that Künzler had drawn. For all his tight jockeying for position, Matthias Künzler found himself facing down 7 creatures with only 2 of his own, and two attack phases later it was all over.

**Matthias Künzler 0 – 1 Marcello Calvetto**

The pull of history had gotten fainter after his first game loss, and seemed to slip away even further as Künzler mulliganed away his first hand, and only reluctantly held onto his second hand of six. Künzler played a second turn [Bitterblossom](https://web.archive.org/web/20220808185906/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Bitterblossom), and on his second turn Calvetto cast two [Thoughtseize](https://web.archive.org/web/20220808185906/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Thoughtseize) to leave the Swiss player with only land in hand! Would the [Bitterblossom](https://web.archive.org/web/20220808185906/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Bitterblossom) be enough?

With nothing better to do Künzler began the aerial assault but Calvetto followed up his devastating second turn with a [Stillmoon Cavalier](https://web.archive.org/web/20220808185906/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Stillmoon+Cavalier) from his sideboard. Künzler [Ponder](https://web.archive.org/web/20220808185906/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Ponder)ed into a [Thoughtseize](https://web.archive.org/web/20220808185906/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Thoughtseize) of his own, and left Calvetto with only a [Broken Ambitions](https://web.archive.org/web/20220808185906/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Broken+Ambitions) and land in hand, then attacked in with his faerie and a [Mutavault](https://web.archive.org/web/20220808185906/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mutavault), capitalising on the four life Calvetto had paid for his [Thoughtseize](https://web.archive.org/web/20220808185906/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Thoughtseize)s and dropping him to 11 life.

![](https://i.ormos.uk/27/a4/27a469c8c7ed08caf296a5d0a030e57697f8ee70094a86e5ad6c599a0099cd28.jpg)With the Italian having mana available to give the [Stillmoon Cavalier](https://web.archive.org/web/20220808185906/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Stillmoon+Cavalier) flying, Künzler sat back and allowed his Fae swarm to grow before attacking again. The Cavalier accounted for the [Mutavault](https://web.archive.org/web/20220808185906/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mutavault), but the faerie tokens got through and Calvetto mistakenly took a point of mana burn. He had given the Cavalier First Strike via a [Sunken Ruins](https://web.archive.org/web/20220808185906/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sunken+Ruins) and forgot to sink the extra mana. Künzler followed up with a second [Bitterblossom](https://web.archive.org/web/20220808185906/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Bitterblossom), and Calvetto fought right back with two [Peppersmoke](https://web.archive.org/web/20220808185906/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Peppersmoke) and a second [Stillmoon Cavalier](https://web.archive.org/web/20220808185906/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Stillmoon+Cavalier)!

Those two [Peppersmoke](https://web.archive.org/web/20220808185906/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Peppersmoke) had utterly transformed the damage race in Calvetto's favour - Künzler was on 9 life, but the Cavaliers and the [Bitterblossom](https://web.archive.org/web/20220808185906/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Bitterblossom) upkeeps would get to him before his remaining Faeries could get to Calvetto... he attacked, putting Calvetto to 5 life but would only have 4 tokens the following turn. Calvetto attacked, reducing Künzler to 4 life.

Matthias Künzler was almost out of the Grand Prix and went for the Hail Mary play, the only play he had left. He cast a [Cryptic Command](https://web.archive.org/web/20220808185906/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cryptic+Command) to draw a card and return an [Island](https://web.archive.org/web/20220808185906/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Island) to Calvetto's hand, leaving him only two mana untapped. Künzler knew the only card in his deck that would help him out was a [Scion of Oona](https://web.archive.org/web/20220808185906/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Scion+of+Oona), and by returning the [Island](https://web.archive.org/web/20220808185906/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Island) he ensured it wouldn't get countered by [Broken Ambitions](https://web.archive.org/web/20220808185906/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Broken+Ambitions). If either of the two cards he would draw were [Scion of Oona](https://web.archive.org/web/20220808185906/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Scion+of+Oona), he would win. But they weren't the saviour that he had prepared for, and Matthias Künzler unhappily extended his hand to concede the match.

**Matthias Künzler 0 – 2 Marcello Calvetto**

After the match, Künzler pondered over whether he had played correctly by casting the second [Bitterblossom](https://web.archive.org/web/20220808185906/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Bitterblossom), and while it was the play that eventually killed him at the time that he cast it, with the information available, it was the right thing to do. The double [Peppersmoke](https://web.archive.org/web/20220808185906/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Peppersmoke) was the killer response, and that was an unlikely scenario to plan for.

History must wait a little longer for an undefeated Grand Prix winner, and it's Marcello Calvetto who advances into the Semi Finals!

## Semifinals – Shuuhei Nakamura (JPN) vs Claudio Salemi (ITA)

*by David Sutcliffe*

![](https://i.ormos.uk/0c/83/0c83890c2b0d436ac5aa30ab4954bd481ab2df23821b7771a44895454ac25b69.jpg) You know that the world has gone mad when Faerie decks are able to play [Doran, the Siege Tower](https://web.archive.org/web/20220808185906/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Doran%2C+the+Siege+Tower) but that was what that faced Shuuhei Nakamura as he looked through Salemi's decklist at the beginning of this semi-final match. Not that Shuuhei's own five-colour Merfolk deck was any less greedy, mind you – such is the reality of the Lorwyn/Shadowmoor block mana bases that the 1UUU of [Cryptic Command](https://web.archive.org/web/20220808185906/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cryptic+Command) can sit comfortably alongside almost any other mana costs. Shuuhei had a more pressing problem, however – his deck appeared to only contain 59 cards!

The missing culprit was a [Nameless Inversion](https://web.archive.org/web/20220808185906/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Nameless+Inversion) that was found among the cards of his quarter-final opponent, and after a few minute's delay to reshuffle we finally got underway. Shuuhei's start was a little tentative, though, leading with a [Stonybrook Banneret](https://web.archive.org/web/20220808185906/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Stonybrook+Banneret) then using a [Cryptic Command](https://web.archive.org/web/20220808185906/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cryptic+Command) to counter Salemi's [Scion of Oona](https://web.archive.org/web/20220808185906/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Scion+of+Oona),and bounce a [Vivid Creek](https://web.archive.org/web/20220808185906/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Vivid+Creek) to hand, giving the Japanese Pro an early mana advantage, which he used to put [Jace Beleren](https://web.archive.org/web/20220808185906/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Jace+Beleren) into play. Nakamura began drawing extra cards from his new Planeswalker ally, cutting down a second [Scion of Oona](https://web.archive.org/web/20220808185906/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Scion+of+Oona) with a [Nameless Inversion](https://web.archive.org/web/20220808185906/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Nameless+Inversion) on the way.

When Nakamura played a [Sygg, River Guide](https://web.archive.org/web/20220808185906/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sygg%2C+River+Guide) his opponent made a critical error, though. Claudio Salemi played a [Spellstutter Sprite](https://web.archive.org/web/20220808185906/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Spellstutter+Sprite) in response but allowed the ability to go onto the stack before he activated a [Mutavault](https://web.archive.org/web/20220808185906/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mutavault) for a second Faerie. A judge confirmed the error, Sygg resolved as a result, and Salemi picked up his permanents and conceded the match. He was obviously disappointed in his own error, but it had never seemed like Salemi had a foothold in the match and seemed to just want to get into a different game as quickly as he could.

**Shuuhei Nakamura 1 - 0 Claudio Salemi**

![](https://i.ormos.uk/f4/c7/f4c79369b7ad6354cf884b19709e471551d154de28fcdf3b71a0572ef33ee68a.jpg)The second game started more brightly for Salemi, and he found a second turn [Bitterblossom](https://web.archive.org/web/20220808185906/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Bitterblossom), only to have Nakamura remove it with a [Wispmare](https://web.archive.org/web/20220808185906/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Wispmare) before he could spawn a single Faerie from it. Shuuhei Nakamura found a little more of his classic Merfolk offense than he had in the first game and began to attack with a [Stonybrook Banneret](https://web.archive.org/web/20220808185906/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Stonybrook+Banneret) and [Silvergill Adept](https://web.archive.org/web/20220808185906/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Silvergill+Adept). Salemi killed the Banneret with a [Nameless Inversion](https://web.archive.org/web/20220808185906/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Nameless+Inversion) and attacked back as best he could with a lone [Scion of Oona](https://web.archive.org/web/20220808185906/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Scion+of+Oona), but his body language was suggesting a player resigned to his fate.

Still, Salemi managed to counter a second [Silvergill Adept](https://web.archive.org/web/20220808185906/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Silvergill+Adept) with a [Spellstutter Sprite](https://web.archive.org/web/20220808185906/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Spellstutter+Sprite) and edge his way back into the damage race, but before he could capitalise on that a [Nameless Inversion](https://web.archive.org/web/20220808185906/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Nameless+Inversion) took down his Scion and after another Japanese attack the Italian was 10-14 down on life. Fighting back, Salemi hit Nakamura with a [Thoughtseize](https://web.archive.org/web/20220808185906/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Thoughtseize) but it revealed a host of power cards – two [Cryptic Command](https://web.archive.org/web/20220808185906/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cryptic+Command) and a [Chameleon Colossus](https://web.archive.org/web/20220808185906/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Chameleon+Colossus). He took a Command, and in Nakamura's next attack traded away both of the attacking creatures for a [Spellstutter Sprite](https://web.archive.org/web/20220808185906/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Spellstutter+Sprite) and a [Crib Swap](https://web.archive.org/web/20220808185906/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Crib+Swap).

That [Crib Swap](https://web.archive.org/web/20220808185906/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Crib+Swap) going away gave Nakamura an excuse to play the [Chameleon Colossus](https://web.archive.org/web/20220808185906/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Chameleon+Colossus) from hand. Salemi had an [Oblivion Ring](https://web.archive.org/web/20220808185906/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Oblivion+Ring) for it, but Nakamura had drawn into a second Colossus! The Italian had no answer for the second Colossus and stuck out his hand to congratulation Shuuhei Nakamura on his win.

**Shuuhei Nakamura 2 – 0 Claudio Salemi**

## Podcast: Sunday, September 14th, 15:30 p.m. – Just Desserts

*by Rich Hagon*

We are done here in Rimini and a blistering Top 8 has left us with a worthy champion. Join us for coverage of some great matches, as Shuuhei Nakamura attempts to deal a massive blow to his rivals in pursuit of the coveted Player of the Year title. Then, set your watches, as Grand Prix: Paris, with Shards of Alara Sealed Deck and Draft, is just a month away. All Magic, all the time, here on magicthegathering.com.  
  
[Click Here to Download](http://webcast2.wizards.com/podcasts/08rimini/gp_rimini_7.mp3)

## Finals – Shuhei Nakamura vs. Emanuele Giusti

*by Tobias Henke*

![](https://i.ormos.uk/ef/12/ef12f2eff535b2dcade0e447de66210a9053eb94a2002c7ca36be25be50a2aaf.jpg)So this is it: The finals; seven-hundred players have been narrowed down to just these two and only one of them will walk away with the trophy. The match-up is one of extreme contrasts: 5-color Merfolk against mono-white Kithkin, far-traveled Pro Tour superstar against local hero...

Nakamura kept a one-land hand, on the draw: [Nameless Inversion](https://web.archive.org/web/20220808185906/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Nameless+Inversion), [Firespout](https://web.archive.org/web/20220808185906/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Firespout), [Jace Beleren](https://web.archive.org/web/20220808185906/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Jace+Beleren), *three*[Silvergill Adept](https://web.archive.org/web/20220808185906/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Silvergill+Adept)s and a [Vivid Creek](https://web.archive.org/web/20220808185906/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Vivid+Creek)...

A pretty good start, provided he draws at least one more land anytime soon. Unfortunately, he didn't. In fact, not a single one showed up over the whole course of this game, which, admittedly, didn't take very long.

**Shuuhei Nakamura 0 – 1 Emanuele Giusti**

The second game started off with a mulligan to six from the Japanese side. Giusti's start, though, was staggeringly slow: No play on turn one, nothing on turn three and no action on turn four. Only turn two saw his [Wizened Cenn](https://web.archive.org/web/20220808185906/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Wizened+Cenn) immediately send to the bin by means of Nakamura's [Nameless Inversion](https://web.archive.org/web/20220808185906/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Nameless+Inversion).

Nevertheless this format's most aggressive beatdown deck does have some big spells (and removal) to make up for slow starts. Not this time though... [Cryptic Command](https://web.archive.org/web/20220808185906/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cryptic+Command) countered [Cloudgoat Ranger](https://web.archive.org/web/20220808185906/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cloudgoat+Ranger) and [Sower of Temptation](https://web.archive.org/web/20220808185906/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sower+of+Temptation) took care (actually, took control of) [Figure of Destiny](https://web.archive.org/web/20220808185906/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Figure+of+Destiny). However, [Spectral Procession](https://web.archive.org/web/20220808185906/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Spectral+Procession) resolved, but one of the tokens found itself enchanted with [Recumbent Bliss](https://web.archive.org/web/20220808185906/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Recumbent+Bliss), to ensure [Windbrisk Heights](https://web.archive.org/web/20220808185906/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Windbrisk+Heights) wouldn't come up with anything dangerous.

Now it was Nakamura's turn to bring on some action: The stolen [Figure of Destiny](https://web.archive.org/web/20220808185906/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Figure+of+Destiny) grew bigger and bigger, and two [Silvergill Adept](https://web.archive.org/web/20220808185906/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Silvergill+Adept)s and a [Stonybrook Banneret](https://web.archive.org/web/20220808185906/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Stonybrook+Banneret) meant that Giusti's remaining tokens stayed home to block. None of them did, though, as one was taken out by [Nameless Inversion](https://web.archive.org/web/20220808185906/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Nameless+Inversion) and the other bounced via [Cryptic Command](https://web.archive.org/web/20220808185906/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cryptic+Command).

Bereft of all creatures, he soon received the final points of damage.

**Shuuhei Nakamura 1 – 1 Emanuele Giusti**

After these two very lopsided games, both players wished for a “nice game” now at least. And indeed, [Figure of Destiny](https://web.archive.org/web/20220808185906/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Figure+of+Destiny) came down on turn one, attacked for two then, and...

...that was the last play for quite some time. Afterwards the game developed into some eloborate sort of staring contest. While Nakamura always had the mana to cast [Nameless Inversion](https://web.archive.org/web/20220808185906/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Nameless+Inversion), Giusti always kept the mana to activate the second ability of his Figure.

Giusti blinked first and to his dismay did indeed lose his little beater. He did have a replacement, though, and not the worst, in form of [Wilt-Leaf Liege](https://web.archive.org/web/20220808185906/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Wilt-Leaf+Liege).

![](https://i.ormos.uk/dd/03/dd03bd04f8ffd5b9410d6f6e63d3f5e9202d6abbcb0db0727b89d5485b9c8655.jpg)[Silvergill Adept](https://web.archive.org/web/20220808185906/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Silvergill+Adept) and [Mutavault](https://web.archive.org/web/20220808185906/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mutavault) entered play on the Japanese side of the table, but it was soon shown that two 2-power guys are not quite as good as one 4/4. When both blocked the Liege, Giusti had [Unmake](https://web.archive.org/web/20220808185906/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Unmake) to remove one of them before the damage went to the stack... but wait! Nakamura had of course accounted for that, and had the [Cryptic Command](https://web.archive.org/web/20220808185906/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cryptic+Command) ready. What he didn't see coming was the *second*[Unmake](https://web.archive.org/web/20220808185906/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Unmake)! He lost both his creatures and soon found himself at six life.

To make matters worse, Giusti added [Figure of Destiny](https://web.archive.org/web/20220808185906/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Figure+of+Destiny) to his team. Looking at his own lands, five of which were of the Vivid variety and one a [Reflecting Pool](https://web.archive.org/web/20220808185906/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Reflecting+Pool), Nakamura played [Sower of Temptation](https://web.archive.org/web/20220808185906/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sower+of+Temptation) at took the Figure of impending awesomeness.

It didn't get that far though. Giusti had [Mirror Entity](https://web.archive.org/web/20220808185906/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mirror+Entity) with four mana up, so the only thing Nakamura could do was cast one and then another [Firespout](https://web.archive.org/web/20220808185906/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Firespout). Giusti immediately restocked with [Spectral Procession](https://web.archive.org/web/20220808185906/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Spectral+Procession). Nakamura emptied his hand (a [Chameleon Colossus](https://web.archive.org/web/20220808185906/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Chameleon+Colossus) among other minor creatures, none of them flying, and a [Jace Beleren](https://web.archive.org/web/20220808185906/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Jace+Beleren)) to the board and passed right back.

Finally, the Italian went for the killing move. Attack with all three tokens, [Mirrorweave](https://web.archive.org/web/20220808185906/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mirrorweave), that's it! Grand Prix-Rimini has found its Champion!

**Shuuhei Nakamura 1 – 1 Emanuele Giusti**

![](https://i.ormos.uk/db/b9/dbb9d5b3ff21085ddf5374520f5be4aee6733dfe22192840f836595ec1c63b66.jpg)
